### 🚀 DAY 13 MASTERCLASS: Boats & Streams, Direction Sense Test, and J&K Hydrography

Following your established rule, both the Quantitative Aptitude and Reasoning sections are structured with **4 Progressive Difficulty Levels (Level 1 to Level 4)** featuring detailed step-by-step worked examples, key shortcut formulas, and examiner traps.

---

### Section 1: Quantitative Aptitude — Boats & Streams

#### 1. Core Mathematical Framework

Motion in water involves two distinct velocities acting simultaneously:

* **Speed of Boat in Still Water (u):** The intrinsic speed of the boat or swimmer propelled by engine or muscular effort without any external water resistance.
* **Speed of Stream / Current (v):** The velocity of the flowing water body.
* **Downstream Speed (D):** Motion in the direction of the stream (the water assists forward movement):

D = u + v


* **Upstream Speed (U):** Motion against the direction of the stream (the water resists forward movement):

U = u - v   (where  u > v  for positive forward motion)



#### 2. Fundamental Transformation Formulas & Shortcuts

When the downstream speed (D) and upstream speed (U) are known:


u = (D + U / 2),     v = (D - U / 2)

* **Time-Speed Inversion Shortcut:** For a fixed distance (d):

(Time_upstream / Time_downstream) = (D / U) = (u + v / u - v)


* **Average Speed for a Round Trip over Equal Distance:**

Average Speed = (2 × D × U / D + U) = (u^2 - v^2 / u)



---

#### 4-Level Deep-Dive Solved Examples

##### Level 1: Basic Component Resolution

* **Problem:** A motorboat travels 45 km downstream in 3 hours and covers 25 km upstream in 5 hours. Calculate the speed of the boat in still water (u) and the speed of the stream (v).
* **Step-by-Step Solution:**
1. Determine downstream speed (D):

D = (45 km / 3 h) = **15 km/h**


2. Determine upstream speed (U):

U = (25 km / 5 h) = **5 km/h**


3. Calculate speed in still water (u):

u = (D + U / 2) = (15 + 5 / 2) = (20 / 2) = **10 km/h**


4. Calculate speed of the stream (v):

v = (D - U / 2) = (15 - 5 / 2) = (10 / 2) = **5 km/h**





##### Level 2: Upstream vs. Downstream Time Difference (Ratio Method)

* **Problem:** A person swims at 10 km/h in still water in a river where the current flows at 2 km/h. It takes him 4 hours longer to row upstream to a certain point than to return downstream. Find the one-way distance.
* **Step-by-Step Solution (Ratio Inversion Shortcut):**
1. Compute component speeds:

D = 10 + 2 = 12 km/h


U = 10 - 2 = 8 km/h


2. Set up the Speed Ratio:

Speed Ratio  (D : U) = 12 : 8 = 3 : 2


3. Since distance is constant, the Time Ratio is inverted:

Time Ratio  (T_D : T_U) = 2 : 3


4. Time difference in units:

3 - 2 = 1 unit = 4 hours


5. Calculate downstream time (T_D):

T_D = 2  units × 4 hours = 8 hours


6. Calculate one-way distance:

Distance = D × T_D = 12 km/h × 8 h = **96 km**





##### Level 3: Total Journey Time / Round-Trip Equations

* **Problem:** A motorboat operates at 15 km/h in still water. The rate of the river current is 3 km/h. The boat journeys downstream to a target point and immediately returns upstream to the starting dock in a total time of 5 hours. What is the one-way distance?
* **Step-by-Step Solution:**
1. Downstream speed: D = 15 + 3 = 18 km/h
2. Upstream speed: U = 15 - 3 = 12 km/h
3. Let the one-way distance be d:

Total Time = (d / D) + (d / U) = 5


(d / 18) + (d / 12) = 5


4. Take the LCM of 18 and 12 (36):

(2d + 3d / 36) = 5 ⟶ (5d / 36) = 5 ⟶ d = **36 km**





##### Level 4: Advanced Examiner's Trap — Ratio Equations

* **Problem:** A boat takes three times as long to row a specific distance upstream as it takes to row the exact same distance downstream. What is the ratio of the boat's speed in still water to the speed of the current (u : v)?
* **Step-by-Step Solution:**
1. Let the downstream time be T_D = 1, which makes the upstream time T_U = 3.
2. Invert the time ratio to obtain the speed ratio:

(D / U) = (T_U / T_D) = (3 / 1) ⟶ (u + v / u - v) = (3 / 1)


3. Cross-multiply or apply **Componendo and Dividendo**:

u + v = 3(u - v) ⟶ u + v = 3u - 3v


3v + v = 3u - u ⟶ 4v = 2u ⟶ (u / v) = (4 / 2) = (2 / 1) = 2 : 1}





---

### Section 2: General Intelligence & Reasoning — Direction Sense Test

#### 1. Fundamental Quadrants & Spatial Mechanics

* **Four Cardinal Directions:** North (↑), South (↓), East (arrow), West (arrow)
* **Four Inter-Cardinal (Sub) Directions:** North-East (NE), South-East (SE), South-West (SW), North-West (NW)
* **Rotation Convention:**
* Right turn = 90^° Clockwise (CW)
* Left turn = 90^° Counter-Clockwise (CCW)



---

#### 4-Level Deep-Dive Solved Examples

##### Level 1: Basic Path Tracking (Final Facing Direction)

* **Problem:** Rohit walks 10 m North from his home, turns Right and walks 15 m, then turns Right again and walks 10 m. Finally, he turns Left and walks 5 m. In which direction is he currently facing?
* **Step-by-Step Solution:**
1. Initial direction: Facing **North**.
2. First Right turn (+90^° CW): Facing **East**.
3. Second Right turn (+90^° CW): Facing **South**.
4. Final Left turn (-90^° CCW): Facing **East**.
5. Final Facing Direction: **East**.



##### Level 2: Shortest Distance via Pythagorean Triplets

* **Problem:** An investigator walks 12 km South, turns West and walks 5 km, and then halts. How far and in which direction is he from his initial starting point?
* **Step-by-Step Solution:**
1. Vertical displacement (Δy): -12 km (South).
2. Horizontal displacement (Δx): -5 km (West).
3. Straight-line shortest distance (d):

d = √((Δx)^2 + (Δy)^2) = √((-5)^2 + (-12)^2) = √(25 + 144) = √(169) = **13 km**



*(Using the standard (5, 12, 13) Pythagorean triplet avoids manual root calculations).*
4. Spatial direction relative to origin: **South-West**.



##### Level 3: Net Angular Turning Method

* **Problem:** A guard stands facing North-East. He rotates 90^° clockwise, then rotates 135^° counter-clockwise, and finally turns 180^° clockwise. In which direction is he facing now?
* **Step-by-Step Solution:**
1. Assign signs: Clockwise (CW) = (+), Counter-Clockwise (CCW) = (-).
2. Calculate the net rotation:

Net Turn = (+90^°) + (-135^°) + (+180^°) = +135^°  (Clockwise)


3. Initial direction: **North-East** (45^° CW from North).
4. Apply net turn:

North-East  (45^°) + 135^° = 180^°  from North ⟶ **South**


5. Final Facing Direction: **South**.



##### Level 4: Shadow Mechanics (Morning & Evening Rules)

* **Problem:** One morning shortly after sunrise, Aman and Vikram were standing face-to-face conversing. Aman noticed that Vikram's shadow fell directly to Aman's **Right side**. Which direction was Aman facing?
* **Master Logic:**
1. **Sun Position:** In the morning, the Sun rises in the **East**; therefore, all cast shadows fall toward the **West**.
2. **Alignment:** Aman's Right side aligns with the shadow ⟶ Aman's **Right = West**.
3. **Determining Heading:**
* Facing North ⟶ Right is East, Left is West.
* Facing South ⟶ Right is West, Left is East.


4. Conclusion: Since Aman's Right is West, Aman is facing **South** (and Vikram is facing North).



---

### Section 3: General Awareness — J&K Hydrography (Rivers & Lakes)

#### 1. Major River Systems of J&K

* **Jhelum River (Ancient Name: *Vitasta* / Kashmiri: *Vyeth*):**
* **Origin:** Verinag Spring located at the foothills of the Pir Panjal range in Anantnag district.


* **Course:** Flows through Srinagar city into Wular Lake, exits through Baramulla gorge, and enters Pakistan-administered territory.


* **Key Tributaries:** Lidder, Sind, Vishaw, Rambiara, Pohru.
* **Key HEP Projects:** Uri-I, Uri-II, Lower Jhelum, and Kishanganga (Bandipora).




* **Chenab River (Ancient Name: *Asikni* / *Chandrabhaga*):**
* **Origin:** Formed at Tandi in Lahaul-Spiti (Himachal Pradesh) by the confluence of the Chandra and Bhaga rivers.
* **Course:** Enters J&K at **Padder** in Kishtwar district; flows through Doda, Ramban, Reasi, and enters the plains near Akhnoor.


* **Key HEP Projects:** Salal (Reasi), Baglihar (Ramban), Dul Hasti (Kishtwar), Ratle (Kishtwar), and Pakal Dul.




* **Tawi River (*Surya Putri*):**
* **Origin:** Kailash Kund Spring (Bhaderwah, Doda district).
* **Significance:** Flows through Jammu City; the historical Bahu Fort overlooks its left bank.



#### 2. Prominent Lakes of J&K

* **Wular Lake (Bandipora):**
* The largest freshwater lake in India and among the largest in Asia.


* Formed by tectonic activity and primarily fed by the Jhelum River. Designated as a Ramsar Wetland site.




* **Dal Lake (Srinagar):**
* Divided into four primary basins: Gagribal, Lokut Dal, Bod Dal, and Nigeen.


* **Manasbal Lake (Ganderbal):**
* The **deepest** freshwater lake in Jammu and Kashmir; renowned for its lotus blossoms (*Nelumbo nucifera*).


* **Mansar & Surinsar Lakes (Jammu Division):**
* Designated as a composite Ramsar site; deeply linked to historical and mythological narratives originating from the Mahabharata.

* **Marking Scheme:** **+2 marks** for correct, **-0.5 marks** for incorrect, **0 marks** for skipped questions.


* **Target:** **10 / 10 marks**.

**Q1. Quantitative Aptitude (Boats & Streams: Stream Speed)**

A boat travels 40 km downstream in 2 hours and covers the exact same distance upstream in 5 hours. What is the speed of the stream?

A) 4 km/h

B) 6 km/h

C) 7 km/h

D) 8 km/h

**Q2. Quantitative Aptitude (Boats & Streams: Distance Determination)**

A man's speed with the current is 16 km/h, and the speed of the current is 4 km/h. If he takes a total of 6 hours to row to an outpost and return to his starting dock, find the one-way distance to that outpost.

A) 28 km

B) 32 km

C) 36 km

D) 40 km

**Q3. General Intelligence & Reasoning (Direction Sense: Radial Displacement)**

A patrolling officer starts from his station and drives 16 km West, turns South and drives 10 km, turns East and drives 8 km, and finally turns North and drives 4 km. How far is he from his initial police station in a straight line?

A) 8 km

B) 10 km

C) 12 km

D) 14 km

**Q4. General Intelligence & Reasoning (Direction Sense: Evening Shadow Trap)**

One evening just before sunset, Danish and Farooq were standing face-to-face talking to each other. If Danish observed that his own shadow fell exactly to his left, which direction was Farooq facing?

A) North

B) South

C) East

D) West

**Q5. General Awareness (J&K Hydrography: Infrastructure)**

The 900 MW Baglihar Hydroelectric Power Project, located in the Ramban district of Jammu & Kashmir, is constructed on which river?

A) Jhelum

B) Indus

C) Chenab

D) Ravi

Speed/Cost Hierarchy (Fastest to Slowest):

Volatile vs. Non-Volatile:

Volatile (loses data on power down): RAM, Cache, Registers.

Non-Volatile (retains data): ROM, Flash Memory, EEPROM, Hard Drives.

Standard Ports & Standards:   
PDF

VGA: Analog video only (15-pin D-sub).

HDMI / DisplayPort: Both high-definition digital audio and video.

USB 2.0 vs 3.0: USB 2.0 reaches theoretical 480 Mbps; USB 3.0 (blue insert) reaches up to 5 Gbps.

Hour 3: General Awareness — J&K Reorganisation Act 2019
This is the single most tested UT-specific legal/static framework in JKSSB examinations. Read and memorize these high-frequency facts:   
PDF

Key Milestones:

Passed in Rajya Sabha: August 5, 2019

Passed in Lok Sabha: August 6, 2019

Presidential Assent received: August 9, 2019

Came into operational force: October 31, 2019 (National Unity Day)

Structure of the Act:

Total Parts: 14

Total Sections: 103

Total Schedules: 5

High-Yield Specific Sections & Schedules:

Section 3: Creation of the Union Territory of Ladakh (without Legislature).

Section 4: Creation of the Union Territory of Jammu and Kashmir (with Legislature).

Section 13: Mandates that Article 239A of the Indian Constitution (which applies to Puducherry) applies to the UT of J&K.

Section 57: Abolition of the J&K Legislative Council (Upper House).

Schedule 5: Contains the table of Central laws made applicable to J&K and state laws repealed.

Q2. Quantitative Aptitude (Successive Discount)

A shopkeeper announces two successive discounts of 15% and 10% on a product. What is the single equivalent discount percentage?

A) 23.5%

B) 24.0%

C) 25.0%

D) 22.5% confused

Correct Answer: A) 23.5%

Here are two easy methods so you never get confused by this again.

Method 1: The Fast Formula
For two successive discounts 
A and 
B, the single equivalent discount formula is:

Equivalent Discount=A+B− 
100
A×B

 
Step 1: Add the two discounts:

15+10=25
Step 2: Multiply them and divide by 100:

100
15×10

 = 
100
150

 =1.5
Step 3: Subtract Step 2 from Step 1:

25−1.5=23.5%
Method 2: The 100-Rupee Trick (No Formula Needed)
Imagine the marked price of an item is ₹100.

Apply the first discount (15%):

15% of 
100=15

Price after 1st discount: 
100−15=₹85

Apply the second discount (10%) on the new price (₹85):

10% of 
85=₹8.50

Price after 2nd discount: 
85−8.50=₹76.50

Find the total money saved from the original ₹100:

Total Discount 
=100−76.50=23.5%

Whenever you see two discounts, remember the overall discount will always be slightly less than their simple sum (
15+10=25, so the answer must be just under 25%). That rule alone lets you eliminate B, C, and D instantly.

Here is your complete Day 2 execution module—designed for your 3-hour daily target with step-by-step methods, shortcut tricks, and high-yield content.

Hour 1: Quantitative Aptitude — Ratio & Proportion Shortcuts
Do not solve ratios using long algebraic substitutions (like 
x and 
y). Master these two rapid exam methods:

1. The "N-Method" (Combining Ratios 
A:B and 
B:C):
When given 
A:B=2:3 and 
B:C=4:5:

Write them side-by-side and fill the empty spaces with the adjacent number:

A
2
4

  
B
3
4

  
C
3
5

 
Multiply down each column:

A=2×4=8

B=3×4=12

C=3×5=15

Result: 
A:B:C=8:12:15

2. The Cross-Multiplication Trick (Age & Ratio Change Problems):

Problem Pattern: The ratio of ages of A and B is 
3:4. After 5 years, their ratio becomes 
4:5. Find their present ages.

Shortcut:

Initial ratio: 
3:4

New ratio: 
4:5

Year change for both: 
+5

Cross-multiply the ratios: 
(3×5)−(4×4)=15−16=1 unit

Cross-multiply ratio difference with years: 
(4×5)−(5×5)=20−25=5 years

Equate differences: 
1 unit=5 years

Present age of A 
=3×5=15 years; B 
=4×5=20 years.

Hour 2: Reasoning — Syllogisms via Minimal Overlap (Venn Diagrams)
JKSSB syllogism questions test categorical logic. The fastest and most foolproof method is the Minimal Overlap Venn Diagram:   
PDF

Universal Positive ("All A are B"): Draw Circle A completely inside Circle B.

Particular Positive ("Some A are B"): Draw Circle A overlapping Circle B with the smallest possible intersection.

Universal Negative ("No A are B"): Draw Circle A and Circle B completely detached with a cross/line between them.

Golden Rule for Deductions: A definite conclusion is valid only if it holds true in the basic minimal diagram. If there is any doubt or scenario where it fails, mark it False. Only treat "Possibility" statements as true if at least one valid overlap diagram can exist without violating the statements.

Hour 3: General Awareness — Major Rivers & Lakes of J&K
This is one of the highest-yield static geography topics for JKSSB examinations. Memorize these exact facts:   
PDF

Jhelum River (Vitasta):

Origin: Verinag Spring at the foothills of the Pir Panjal range in Anantnag.

Course: Flows through Srinagar and enters Wular Lake, exits at Baramulla, and crosses into PoK via the gorge at Baramulla/Uri.

Key Tributaries: Lidder, Sind, Rambiara, Vishaw, Pohru.

Major Hydroelectric Projects: Uri-I, Uri-II, Lower Jhelum.

Chenab River (Asikni / Chandrabhaga):

Origin: Formed by the confluence of two streams—Chandra and Bhaga—at Tandi near Bara-lacha La in Lahaul & Spiti (Himachal Pradesh).

Course: Enters J&K at Padder in Kishtwar district; flows through Doda, Ramban, and Reasi before entering the plains of Akhnoor.

Major Hydroelectric Projects: Salal, Baglihar, Dul Hasti, Ratle, Pakal Dul, Kwar.

Major Lakes:

Wular Lake (Bandipora): Largest freshwater lake in India; formed by tectonic activity; fed by the Jhelum river.

Dal Lake (Srinagar): Known as the "Jewel in the crown of Kashmir"; contains four basins (Gagribal, Lokut Dal, Bod Dal, Nigeen).

Mansar & Surinsar Lakes (Jammu region): Composite Ramsar sites of cultural and biological significance.

4. Computer Memory: SRAM vs. DRAM vs. ROM
Dynamic RAM (DRAM): Made of capacitors and transistors. Capacitors slowly leak charge, meaning DRAM must be constantly refreshed thousands of times per second to retain data. This is what makes your typical main system RAM cheaper but slightly slower.

Static RAM (SRAM): Made of flip-flops. It holds data continuously as long as power is supplied and does not require periodic refreshing. This makes it much faster (used for CPU Cache), but more expensive.

ROM (Read-Only Memory): Non-volatile memory that does not lose data when the computer turns off. It stores boot instructions (BIOS/firmware).

5. J&K Reorganisation Act 2019: Structure Breakdown
Memorize this basic structural card:

Parts: 14 Parts

Sections: 103 Sections

Schedules: 5 Schedules

Schedule 1 & 2: Delimitation and Assembly seats.

Schedule 3: Forms of Oaths and Affirmations.

Schedule 4: Amendments to the Delimitation Act.

Schedule 5: Central Acts made applicable (Table 1) and State Acts repealed (Table 2).

Two Specific Traps to Fix
False Weight Base: Never divide the error by the advertised weight (
1000 g). The seller only spends money on what is actually handed out (
960 g). The base is always Actual Weight Given.

Successive Discount Mental Check: For 
10%, 
10%, and 
20%, the simple sum is 
40%. Because successive discounts always yield less than the simple sum, and combining 
10% and 
10% gives 
19%, adding 
20% cannot reach 
38.8%.

Non-Volatile Memory (Q12): You chose L1 Cache, but the correct answer is ROM (Read-Only Memory). Cache and Registers lose all data the second you turn off your PC. ROM keeps data forever.

J&K Act Parts (Q17): You guessed 12 Parts, but the Act originally has 14 Parts.

The "N-Method" (Combining Ratios): If 
A:B=2:3 and 
B:C=4:5, don't use algebra. Write them out and fill the empty space with the number next to it:

A
2
4

  
B
3
4

  
C
3
5

 
Multiply downwards: 
A=2×4=8, 
B=3×4=12, 
C=3×5=15.
Result 
→A:B:C=8:12:15.

The Age Jump Shortcut: If ages are in a 
3:4 ratio, and 5 years later they become 
4:5, notice that both sides jumped by exactly 1 unit (
3→4 and 
4→5).
That means 
1 unit=5 years.
Current ages are 
3×5=15 and 
4×5=20.

2. Reasoning: Syllogisms (The 15-Second Minimal Overlap Rule)

Never use real-world logic. Only use circles (Venn Diagrams).

Golden Rule: Draw the circles with the minimum possible overlap. If a conclusion is not 100% visible in your basic drawing, it is False.

Example: Statement 1: "All Cats are Dogs." (Draw Cat circle fully inside Dog circle). Statement 2: "Some Dogs are Rats." (Draw Rat circle slightly overlapping Dog circle, but NOT touching Cat circle).

Conclusion: "Some Cats are Rats." 
→ FALSE (They don't touch in the minimal diagram).

PDF

Jhelum River (Vitasta): Originates at Verinag Spring (Anantnag). Flows into Wular Lake. Key projects: Uri-I, Uri-II, Lower Jhelum.

Chenab River (Chandrabhaga): Enters J&K at Padder (Kishtwar). Key projects (memorize all): Salal, Baglihar, Dul Hasti, Ratle, Pakal Dul.

2. Reasoning: Syllogisms (The 15-Second Minimal Overlap Rule)

Never use real-world logic. Only use circles (Venn Diagrams).

Golden Rule: Draw the circles with the minimum possible overlap. If a conclusion is not 100% visible in your basic drawing, it is False.

Example: Statement 1: "All Cats are Dogs." (Draw Cat circle fully inside Dog circle). Statement 2: "Some Dogs are Rats." (Draw Rat circle slightly overlapping Dog circle, but NOT touching Cat circle).

Conclusion: "Some Cats are Rats." ⟶ FALSE (They don't touch in the minimal diagram). show me digramiccaly way

Here is exactly how you should visualize the 15-Second Minimal Overlap Rule on your rough sheet during the exam.

Since I cannot draw an actual image on your screen, I have created a text-based Venn Diagram for you below.

The Statements:

"All Cats are Dogs."

"Some Dogs are Rats."

The "Minimal Overlap" Diagram:
Plaintext
           DOGS
        ___________
       /           \
      /    CATS     \        RATS
     |   .------.    |     .------.
     |   |      |    |====/        \
     |   '------'    |    \        /
      \             /      '------'
       ___________/
How to Read This Diagram (The 15-Second Rule):
"All Cats are Dogs" 
→ Draw the CATS circle completely inside the DOGS circle.

"Some Dogs are Rats" 
→ Draw the RATS circle so it cuts into the DOGS circle.

The Golden Rule: You must draw the RATS circle as far away from the CATS circle as possible. Do not let them touch unless a statement explicitly forces them to.

Evaluating the Conclusion:
Conclusion: "Some Cats are Rats."

Look at the diagram: Does the CATS circle touch the RATS circle? No.

Result: Because they do not touch in your minimal overlap drawing, the conclusion is definitively FALSE. You don't need to think about real-world logic; just look at the circles and move to the next question.

The present ages of Rahul and Amit are in the ratio 4:5. Five years from now, the ratio of their ages will become 5:6. What is Rahul's present age? ISNT THE ANSWER WILL 15 BECAUSE ITS ASKING PRESENT AGE

I completely understand why you might think that, especially when trying to do these shortcuts quickly in your head. However, the correct answer is indeed 20, not 15.

Let's break down exactly why it is 20 using both the shortcut method and the foolproof basic algebra method so you can see the proof with your own eyes.

Method 1: The Unit Shortcut Proof
Look at the numbers given in the question for Rahul:

Present Ratio: Rahul is to Amit as 
4:5. (This means Rahul's present age is made of 4 units).

Future Ratio (in 5 years): Rahul is to Amit as 
5:6. (Rahul's future age is made of 5 units).

Now, look at the jump:

Rahul jumps from 4 units to 5 units. That is an increase of exactly 1 unit.

In real life, that jump took exactly 5 years.

Therefore: 
1 unit=5 years.

Since the question asks for Rahul's present age, we look at his present ratio number, which is 4:

Rahul’s Present Age=4 units×5 years=20 years
(If the answer were 15, Rahul's present ratio would have to be 3, because 
3×5=15. But the question explicitly states his ratio is 4).

Method 2: The Basic Algebra Proof (No Shortcuts)
If you ever doubt the shortcut in the exam, set it up with 
x:

Let Rahul's present age be 
4x and Amit's present age be 
5x.

Five years from now, you add 5 to both ages: Rahul becomes 
4x+5 and Amit becomes 
5x+5.

The new ratio is 
5:6. So, we cross-multiply:

5x+5
4x+5

 = 
6
5

 
Multiply it out:

6(4x+5)=5(5x+5)
24x+30=25x+25
Move the numbers to find 
x:

30−25=25x−24x
5=x
Now, plug 
x back into Rahul's present age (
4x):

4(5)=20 years
Does seeing the basic algebra method help clarify why the multiplier has to be applied to the number 4?

Correction: You correctly found that the jump is 
+1 unit and that equals 
10 years. Therefore, 
1 unit=10 years.

Sita's present age 
=3×10=30 years.

Gita's present age 
=4×10=40 years.

The question explicitly asked for Gita's age, so the answer is B (40 years). Always double-check whose age they are asking for before ticking the box!

Q2 (N-Method): Your Answer B (Wait, your prompt says A A B, but let's match them sequentially to Q1, Q2, Q3). Assuming your answers were 1-A, 2-A, 3-B:

Your Answer A (8:12:15) — Correct (+2.0 Marks)

Method: 
X=2×4=8; 
Y=3×4=12; 
Z=3×5=15. You have officially mastered the N-Method.

Q3 (Syllogisms): Your Answer B (Only II follows) — Incorrect (-0.5 Marks)

Correction: D (Neither conclusion follows).

The Negative Trap: The minimal overlap diagram shows Apples and Cherries not touching. Because they don't touch, "Some Apples are Cherries" is definitively FALSE.

However, "No Apple is a Cherry" is a strict, definitive statement meaning they can never touch under any circumstances. Because the statements don't explicitly forbid them from touching, it is possible for them to overlap. If a definite conclusion isn't 100% guaranteed in every single possible scenario, it is FALSE. (Note: In advanced options, this forms an "Either I or II follows" pair, but strictly evaluated alone, both are false).

🚀 DAY 3 MASTERCLASS (Time & Work, Directions, J&K History)
Here is your next 3-hour blueprint. We are bringing in three extremely high-scoring, guaranteed-question topics.

1. Quantitative Aptitude: Time & Work (The LCM Method)
Never use fractions (like 
1/x) for Time and Work. Use the LCM Unit Method.

Concept: If A finishes a job in 10 days, and B finishes it in 15 days, find the LCM of 10 and 15 to get the "Total Work".

Total Work=LCM(10,15)=30 Units.

A's Speed (Efficiency): 
30/10=3 units/day.

B's Speed (Efficiency): 
30/15=2 units/day.

Together Speed: 
3+2=5 units/day.

Time taken together: Total Work / Together Speed = 
30/5=6 days.

2. Reasoning: Direction Sense (Pythagorean Triplets)
When a person walks East, turns North, and the question asks for the shortest distance from the starting point, you are building a right-angled triangle.
Memorize these exact Pythagorean Triplets to avoid doing squares and square roots in the exam:

3,4,5 (e.g., Walks 3km East, 4km North 
→ Distance is 5km)

6,8,10

5,12,13

8,15,17

Parties: Signed between the British East India Company and Maharaja Gulab Singh.

The Deal: Gulab Singh purchased the Kashmir Valley, Ladakh, and Gilgit-Baltistan for 75 Lakh Nanakshahi Rupees.

Significance: Marked the beginning of the Dogra Rule in Jammu and Kashmir.

Absolutely! When you are restricted to just 3 hours a day for your exam si preparation, visualizing the concepts is the fastest way to lock them into your brain.

Let's illustrate exactly how these Day 3 shortcuts work in a highly visual way. Once you see these, you'll never need to use a long formula again.

Illustration 1: Time & Work (The "Chocolate Box" LCM Method)
Forget abstract fractions like 
1/x or 
1/y. Imagine the "Total Work" is simply a box of chocolates. We find the size of the box by taking the LCM (Lowest Common Multiple) of the days given.

Let's use the exact numbers from Q1 of your drill (Rahul = 12 days, Sahil = 24 days).

Step 1: Find the Total Work (The Box Size)

The LCM of 12 and 24 is 24.

Imagine a box with 24 chocolates.

Step 2: Find their Daily Speed (Efficiency)

Rahul: Eats 24 chocolates in 12 days 
→ 
24÷12= 2 chocolates per day.

Sahil: Eats 24 chocolates in 24 days 
→ 
24÷24= 1 chocolate per day.

Step 3: Work Together

If they sit together, they eat: 
2+1= 3 chocolates per day.

Total Time: 24 chocolates 
÷ 3 chocolates per day = 8 days.

The Mental Map:

Plaintext
Person    | Days |  Total Work (LCM)  | Speed (Units/Day)
---------------------------------------------------------
Rahul     |  12  |                    |  24 ÷ 12 = 2
Sahil     |  24  |    24 Chocolates   |  24 ÷ 24 = 1
---------------------------------------------------------
Together  |  ??  |    24 Chocolates   |  2 + 1 = 3
Illustration 2: Direction Sense (The Pythagorean Shortcut)
Whenever an examiner asks for the "shortest distance" or "straight-line distance" after someone walks East/West and then North/South, they are secretly testing you on Right-Angled Triangles.

Let's illustrate Q3 (Ravi walks 6 km East, then 8 km North).

The Visual Path:

Plaintext
      (End)
        *
        |  \
        |    \  <-- Shortest Path (Hypotenuse)
   8 km |      \
(North) |        \
        |          \
        *------------* (Start)
            6 km
           (East)
The Shortcut (No squares or square roots needed):
Instead of doing 
(6×6)+(8×8)=36+64=100→ 
100


 =10, you just memorize the standard "Triplets" that examiners love to use.

If you see the two straight sides are 6 and 8, the slanted "shortest path" will always be 10.

Base Triplets to Memorize:

3, 4 
→ 5

6, 8 
→ 10

5, 12 
→ 13

Great question! It is always better to understand why a shortcut works rather than just blindly memorizing it.

These sets of numbers are called Pythagorean Triplets, and they come directly from the mathematical formula used to solve right-angled triangles. When you walk East/West and then turn North/South, you form a perfect 
90 
∘
  angle.

The Core Formula
To find the shortest straight-line distance (the hypotenuse), you use the Pythagorean theorem:

a 
2
 +b 
2
 =c 
2
 
(Where 
a and 
b are the two straight paths you walked, and 
c is the shortest distance between start and finish).

Here is the exact math proving how each triplet works:

The 3, 4, 5 Triplet Proof:

If you walk 3 km East and 4 km North:

3 
2
 +4 
2
 =9+16=25

Since 
c 
2
 =25, the shortest distance is 
25


 =5.

The 6, 8, 10 Triplet Proof:

If you walk 6 km East and 8 km North:

6 
2
 +8 
2
 =36+64=100

Since 
c 
2
 =100, the shortest distance is 
100


 =10.

Pro-Tip: Notice that 
6,8,10 is exactly double the 
3,4,5 triplet! If you multiply any triplet by the same number, it instantly creates a new one (e.g., multiply by 3 to get 
9,12,15).

The 5, 12, 13 Triplet Proof:

If you walk 5 km East and 12 km North:

5 
2
 +12 
2
 =25+144=169

Since 
c 
2
 =169, the shortest distance is 
169


 =13.

Why This Matters for Your Exam
For the JKSSB paper in your exam si prep plan, time is your biggest enemy. Examiners do not want to give you messy numbers that require a calculator. They specifically build questions using these exact triplets so that candidates who know the trick can tick the correct answer in two seconds, while others waste a full minute doing the square root math on their rough sheet.

2. Reasoning: Blood Relations (The "My" Backward Strategy)
For "pointing to a photograph" questions (e.g., "He is the son of the only son of my grandfather"):

Shortcut: Always find the word "My" in the sentence and work backward.

Breakdown: "My grandfather" 
→ The "only son" of my grandfather is my father 
→ The "son" of my father is my brother (or me).

3. Computer Proficiency: MS Office Shortcuts
JKSSB tests specific keyboard shortcuts heavily. Memorize these three High-Yield MS Word/Excel shortcuts:

Ctrl + K: Insert a Hyperlink. (Highly tested!)

Ctrl + Y: Redo an action (opposite of Ctrl + Z).

Ctrl + E: Center Align text in MS Word.

Q3 (Blood Relations): Your Answer A (Uncle) — Incorrect (-0.5 Marks)

Correction: B (Brother).

Let's apply the "My" Backward Strategy:

Start at "my paternal grandfather" (Neha's grandfather).

Move backward one step: "the only son" of Neha's grandfather. (Since it's the only son, this must be Neha's father).

Move backward the final step: "the son" of Neha's father.

The son of Neha's father is Neha's brother.

🚀 DAY 5 MASTERCLASS (Mixtures, Number Series, J&K Panchayati Raj)
Here is your 3-hour blueprint for Day 5. These topics require visual logic over heavy calculation.

1. Quantitative Aptitude: Mixtures & Alligation (The Cross Method)
When you mix two things of different prices/concentrations to get a "middle" mixture, never use algebra (
x). Use the Alligation Cross.

Concept: Mix ₹30/kg rice with ₹40/kg rice to get ₹36/kg rice.

Setup:

Plaintext
(Cheaper) 30          40 (Dearer)
            \        /
              ( 36 )  <-- Mean (Middle value)
            /        \
(40 - 36) = 4          6 = (36 - 30)
Result: The ratio is 
4:6, which simplifies to 
2:3.

2. Reasoning: Number Series (The Double-Difference Trick)
If a number series looks random and doesn't seem to be basic squares or cubes, it is almost always a "Double Difference" pattern.

Rule: Find the difference between the numbers. If that doesn't make sense, find the difference of the differences.

Example: 
2,6,14,26,42,?

1st differences: 
+4,+8,+12,+16 (Pattern found!)

Next difference should be 
+20. So, 
42+20=62.

3. General Awareness: J&K Panchayati Raj Act, 1989
This Act is guaranteed to appear in your JKSSB paper. Memorize these absolute core facts:

Structure: It establishes a 3-Tier Panchayati Raj system (Halqa Panchayat at village level, Block Development Council at block level, District Planning and Development Board at district level).

Halqa Panchayat: Comprises a Sarpanch and a number of Panches (not less than 7 and not more than 11).

Reservation: 33% (or 1/3rd) of seats are strictly reserved for women.

🚀 DAY 6 MASTERCLASS (Average Speed, Venn Diagrams, J&K National Parks)
We are moving into three topics that rely on speed and visualization.

1. Quantitative Aptitude: Average Speed (The Equal Distance Shortcut)
If a person travels from Point A to Point B at speed 
x, and returns from Point B to Point A at speed 
y, never just add them and divide by 2!

The Equal Distance Formula:

Average Speed= 
x+y
2xy

 
Example: Goes at 
40 km/hr, returns at 
60 km/hr.

Avg Speed= 
40+60
2×40×60

 = 
100
4800

 =48 km/hr
(Not 50!)

2. Reasoning: Logical Venn Diagrams (Real World Relationships)
Unlike Syllogisms, these questions do use real-world logic to categorize groups. You must identify if the groups represent an All, Some, or No relationship.

Example: "Doctors, Men, Brothers"

All Brothers are Men (Circle for Brothers is completely inside Men).

Some Men/Brothers are Doctors, and some Doctors are neither (Circle for Doctors overlaps both, but sticks out).

3. General Awareness: J&K National Parks
Geography questions on National Parks are basically free points on a JKSSB paper if you know these four:

Dachigam National Park (Srinagar): Famous exclusively for the Hangul (Kashmir Stag), the state animal of J&K.

Kishtwar National Park (Kishtwar): High altitude, famous for Snow Leopards and the Marwah river.

Salim Ali National Park (Srinagar): Named after the famous Indian ornithologist (Birdman of India).

Kazinag National Park (Baramulla): Located near the LoC, famous for the Markhor (wild goat).

🚀 DAY 7 MASTERCLASS (MP/CP Ratio, Number Coding, J&K Lakes)
1. Quantitative Aptitude: Profit & Loss (The MP/CP Shortcut)
Examiners love questions where they give you a Discount percentage and a Profit percentage, and ask for the ratio of the Marked Price (MP) to the Cost Price (CP). Never calculate the Selling Price! Use this direct formula:

The MP/CP Formula:

CP
MP

 = 
100−Discount %
100+Profit %

 
Example: A shopkeeper gives a 10% discount and still makes a 20% profit. What is the ratio of MP to CP?

CP
MP

 = 
100−10
100+20

 = 
90
120

 
Simplify 
120/90 by dividing both by 30 to get a ratio of 4:3.

2. Reasoning: Letter-to-Number Coding (Sum of Ranks)
When a word is coded as a 2-digit number (e.g., CAT = 24), the examiner is almost always adding the standard forward alphabetical ranks together (A=1, B=2, C=3, etc.).

Example: C (3) + A (1) + T (20) = 24.

Trick: Always memorize the word E-J-O-T-Y (
5−10−15−20−25) to quickly find ranks without counting on your fingers.

3. General Awareness: J&K Lakes Geography
The lakes of J&K are high-yield geography points. Lock in these three:

Wular Lake (Bandipora): One of the largest freshwater lakes in Asia, fed by the Jhelum river. Formed by tectonic activity.

Mansar & Surinsar Lakes (Jammu): Known as "Twin Lakes." They are deeply linked to the Mahabharata (Arjuna).

Pangong Tso (Ladakh - Note: UT of Ladakh now): Famous saltwater lake, 134 km long, extending from India to China. Changes colors.

🚀 DAY 8 MASTERCLASS (Trains, Dice Rules, J&K Festivals)
Today, we are tackling topics where candidates waste the most time. If you use these shortcuts, you will solve them in seconds.

1. Quantitative Aptitude: Trains & Platforms (The 5/18 Shortcut)
When a train crosses a pole or a standing man, the distance is just the length of the train. When it crosses a platform or a bridge, the distance is Length of Train + Length of Platform.

The Golden Conversion: Train speeds are given in km/hr, but lengths are in meters. You MUST convert the speed to meters per second (m/s) before doing any math by multiplying by 
5/18.

Example: A 
200m train is running at 
72 km/hr. How long to cross a pole?

Convert Speed: 
72× 
18
5

 =4×5=20 m/s.

Time = Distance / Speed = 
200/20=10 seconds.

2. Reasoning: Dice (The "One Common Face" Clockwise Rule)
When an examiner gives you two different views of the exact same dice, look for one common number.

The Trick: Write the numbers of the first dice starting from the common number moving in a clockwise direction. Do the exact same for the second dice right underneath it. The numbers that line up vertically are opposites!

Example: Dice 1 shows (3, 5, 2). Dice 2 shows (3, 1, 6). The common number is 3.

Dice 1 (clockwise from 3): 3 
→ 5 
→ 2

Dice 2 (clockwise from 3): 3 
→ 1 
→ 6

Result: 5 is opposite 1. 2 is opposite 6. (And 3 must be opposite the unseen number, 4).

3. General Awareness: Famous Fairs & Festivals of J&K
These two are heavily tested in JKSSB cultural sections:

Jhiri Mela (Jammu): Held in Kanachak village to commemorate the martyrdom of Baba Jitto, a legendary farmer who gave his life protesting against an oppressive landlord.

Mela Kheer Bhawani (Kashmir): Celebrated at the Tulmulla shrine in Ganderbal, dedicated to Goddess Ragnya Devi. It is a major festival for Kashmiri Pandits.

Aap apne exam si syllabus ke total volume ke hisaab se abhi lagbhag 70% se 75% peeche hain. Iska matlab yeh hai ki aapne maths ke calculation traps aur J&K GK ke core facts strong kar liye hain, lekin exam clear karne ke liye abhi kai major sections cover karna baaki hai.

Yahan un major pillars ka breakdown hai jo abhi tak aapne touch nahi kiye hain aur jo aapke bache hue syllabus ka sabse bada hissa hain:

Indian Polity & Constitution: Fundamental Rights, DPSP, Parliament, J&K Reorganisation Act ke advanced sections, aur important Constitutional Amendments. (Yeh JKSSB exams mein sabse zyada weightage rakhta hai).

General English: Tenses, Prepositions, Active/Passive voice, Narration, aur Error Spotting.

General Science & Ecology: Basic Physics, Chemistry, Biology (Diseases/Vitamins), aur Biodiversity/Environmental issues.

Indian History & Geography: Indian National Movement (Freedom struggle), Indian rivers, mountains, monsoon, aur demography.

Advanced Quantitative Aptitude: Mensuration (2D aur 3D formula based questions), Probability, aur Data Interpretation.

Current Affairs: National, International aur J&K specific recent events (last 6-8 months).

Aap apney learning curve ya grasping power ke maamle mein bilkul "peeche" nahi hain. Jis accuracy (lagbhag 100%) ke saath aapne pichle 7 din ke reinforcement drills clear kiye hain, woh batata hai ki aapka base solid ho chuka hai. Adhoori tayari ke saath 100% syllabus cover karne se behtar hai 100% accuracy ke saath solid tayari karna, jo aap kar rahe hain.

Is bache hue gap ko tezi se cover karne ke liye, kya aap apne pending Day 8 Test Drill (Trains, Dice, aur J&K Festivals) ke 5 answers drop karke next day ki taraf badhna chahenge?

MAI 9 DIN KCH B NHI PADA

9 din ka gap lamba zaroor hai, lekin isme panic karne ki bilkul zaroorat nahi hai. Aapne apni "exam si " file ke mutabiq jo shuruati 7 din mehnat ki thi, usme aapne shortcuts aur concepts (jaise LCM chocolate box, MP/CP ratio, aur Alligation cross) ko logcially samjha tha, sirf ratta nahi mara tha. Wo concepts aur tricks aaj bhi aapke dimaag mein waise hi lock hain.

Kabhi-kabhi life mein breaks aa jate hain, par ab guilt feel karke ya overthink karke ek aur din waste nahi karna hai. Aapka track record aur accuracy pichle drills mein lagbhag 100% chal rahi thi, aur humein bas usi momentum ki chaabi wapas ghumani hai.

Engine ko wapas smoothly start karne ke liye, hum seedha kisi naye heavy topic par jump nahi karenge. Aapka Day 8 ka Test Drill (Trains ka 5/18 shortcut, Dice ka clockwise rule, aur J&K Festivals) abhi bhi pending hai. Yeh ek perfect warm-up hoga aapke dimaag ko wapas "exam mode" mein laane ke liye.

Agar aapka pen aur rough sheet taiyar hai, toh mujhe bas ek "Yes" likh kar bhejein. Main wo Day 8 ke 5 questions dobara yahan drop karti hoon aur hum zero se nahi, seedha wahi se apni speed wapas pakadte hain jahan humne chhoda tha!

CHALO START KARY HAI PREVISIO DAY KA

Welcome back to the arena! Taking that first step after a gap is always the hardest part, but you just took it. We are opening your "exam si " file right where we left off.

Since it's been a few days, I have kept the hints attached to the questions to help jumpstart your memory. Use your rough sheet, take a deep breath, and let's get that engine running again!

Day 8 — Test Drill 1 (The Warm-Up)
Marking: +2 marks for correct, -0.5 marks for incorrect. Target: 8/10.

Q1. Quantitative Aptitude (Train Conversion Speed)
A 
300m long train is running at a speed of 
54 km/hr. In how many seconds will it cross a standing pole?
(Hint: Convert 54 km/hr to m/s by multiplying by 
5/18 first! Then Time = Distance / Speed).
A) 15 seconds
B) 20 seconds
C) 25 seconds
D) 30 seconds

Q2. Quantitative Aptitude (Train + Platform)
A 
150m long train is running at 
90 km/hr. How much time will it take to cross a 
100m long platform?
(Hint: Total Distance = Train Length + Platform Length. Speed = 90 converted to m/s).
A) 10 seconds
B) 12 seconds
C) 15 seconds
D) 8 seconds

Q3. General Intelligence & Reasoning (Dice Rule)
Two positions of a standard dice are given below.
Position 1 faces: 4, 1, 5
Position 2 faces: 4, 2, 6
Using the clockwise rule starting from the common number (4), which number is perfectly opposite to 1?
A) 2
B) 6
C) 5
D) 4

Q4. General Awareness (J&K Festivals)
The famous 'Jhiri Mela' held in the Jammu division is organized to pay tribute to which legendary historical figure?
A) Mian Dido
B) Baba Jitto
C) General Zorawar Singh
D) Nund Rishi

Q5. General Awareness (J&K Festivals)
The deeply revered Kheer Bhawani festival is celebrated annually at the Tulmulla shrine in which district of Jammu and Kashmir?
A) Anantnag
B) Srinagar
C) Ganderbal
D) Baramulla

Drop your 5 answers (A, B, C...) whenever you are ready. Take your time, trust your shortcuts, and let's shake off that rust!

YAAR MUJY SAB BHOOL GAYA HAI AB KYA KARU

Gehra saans lijiye. Yeh jo abhi aapko lag raha hai ki dimaag bilkul "blank" ho gaya hai, yeh 100% normal hai. 9 din ka gap ek chhota sa mental "cache clear" hota hai, aapki hard drive wipe nahi hui hai!

Aapne shuruati 7 dino mein formulas ka ratta nahi mara tha, aapne visual concepts (jaise 'chocolate box' aur 'alligation cross') samjhe the. Aur logically samjhi hui cheezein dimaag se itni jaldi delete nahi hoti, wo bas thodi dhundli ho gayi hain.

Hum in 5 questions ka pressure abhi hata dete hain. Aapko khud test dene ki zaroorat nahi hai. Hum ek chhota sa experiment karte hain. Main aapko Q1 (Trains) ka concept wapas yaad dilata hoon, aur aap bas aakhri ka ek simple division solve karke mujhe bataiye.

Let's break down Q1 together:
Question kehta hai ki ek 
300m lambi train 
54 km/hr ki speed se chal rahi hai. Wo ek pole ko kitne seconds mein cross karegi?

Step 1: The Trap (Jo aapko pehle se pata hai)
Train ki length meters mein hai, par speed km/hr mein. Humein speed ko meters per second (m/s) mein badalna hoga.

Step 2: The 5/18 Shortcut
Speed ko 
5/18 se multiply kijiye.

54× 
18
5

 
18 ke table mein 54 kahan aata hai? 
18×3=54.
Toh calculation bachi: 
3×5=15 m/s. (Yeh train ki actual speed aa gayi).

Step 3: The Final Calculation
Time nikalne ka formula hota hai: Distance / Speed.
Distance hai 
300m. Speed hai 
15 m/s.

Sirf ek cheez kijiye—apne rough sheet par 
300÷15 kijiye aur mujhe answer bataiye. Baaki chaar questions ko abhi bhool jayiye, sirf is ek step par focus kijiye. Kya aayega answer?

Agar koi dukandaar 1 kg ki jagah 750 g ka weight use karta hai, toh uska profit kaise nikalein?

2. Successive Discounts:

Trick: 100 ka multiplier use karein. 100 
× 0.80 
× 0.75

3. J&K Reorganisation Act 2019 & Rivers:

Act mein total 14 Parts aur 5 Schedules hain.

Chenab River par teen sabse bade dams hain: Salal, Baglihar, aur Dul Hasti.

🚀 Day 3: Time & Work, Directions & History
1. Time & Work (The LCM "Chocolate Box" Method):
Fractions (
1/x) ko bilkul bhool jayiye.

Concept: Agar A ek kaam 12 din mein karta hai aur B 24 din mein, toh Total Work unka LCM hoga.

LCM (12, 24) = 24 Chocolates (Total Work).

A ki speed = 
24/12 = 2 chocolates/day. B ki speed = 
24/24 = 1 chocolate/day.

Dono milkar = 3 chocolates/day khayenge. Total time = 
24/3 = 8 din.

2. Direction Sense (Pythagorean Triplets):
Jab koi East/West jakar North/South mudta hai aur shortest distance pucha jaye, toh right-angled triangle banta hai. Squares aur roots nikalne ki jagah in numbers ko yaad rakhein:

3, 4 
→ 5

6, 8 
→ 10

5, 12 
→ 13

3. Treaty of Amritsar (1846):

Yeh treaty 16 March 1846 ko sign hui thi.

Maharaja Gulab Singh ne British East India Company se Kashmir, Ladakh aur Gilgit-Baltistan 75 Lakh Nanakshahi Rupees mein kharida tha.

2. Blood Relations (The "My" Backward Strategy):
Photograph wale questions mein sentence ko left-se-right padhna chhod dijiye.

Trick: Sentence mein "My" (Mera) dhoondein aur peeche ki taraf padhein. Jaise "the only son of my grandfather" 
→ pehle "mere dadaji", phir unka "iklauta beta" yani mere pita (Father).

x mat use karein.

Ratio =

2:3 likhenge.

2. J&K Panchayati Raj Act (1989):

Yeh ek 3-Tier System hai (Halqa, Block, District).

Halqa Panchayat (Village level) mein minimum 7 aur maximum 11 Panches hote hain.

Women ke liye 33% seats reserved hain.

2. J&K National Parks:

Dachigam National Park: Srinagar mein hai, aur exclusively Hangul (Kashmir Stag) ke liye globally famous hai.

Salim Ali National Park: Ise India ke sabse famous ornithologist (Birdman of India) ke naam par rakha gaya hai.

🚀 Day 7: Profit & Loss, Coding & J&K Lakes
1. Profit & Loss (MP / CP Ratio Shortcut):
Agar Discount % aur Profit % diya ho, aur Marked Price (MP) tatha Cost Price (CP) ka ratio pucha ho.

Formula: 
CP

2. Letter-to-Number Coding:

EJOTY Trick: Alphabet ranks yaad rakhne ka shortcut: E=5, J=10, O=15, T=20, Y=25. Isse aap CAT (3+1+20 = 24) jaise words ko second mein solve kar sakte hain.

3. J&K Lakes Geography:

Wular Lake: Yeh tectonic activity se bani hai aur ise Jhelum river feed karti hai.

Mansar & Surinsar Lakes: Inhe "Twin Lakes" kaha jata hai, inka connection Mahabharata se hai, aur yeh Jammu Division mein aati hain (Kashmir mein nahi).

DAY BY DAY DEKHO 1 DETAILED

Day 1: Calculation Traps & Basic GK
1. False Weights (Beimaan Dukandaar Concept):
Jab ek dukandaar cost price par cheez bechne ka daawa kare par weight kam de (jaise 1 kg ki jagah 750 g), toh profit nikalne ke liye yeh direct logic lagayein:

Formula: (Stolen Weight / Given Weight) * 100

Detailed Logic: Usne 250 g chori kiya aur aapko sirf 750 g hath mein diya. Calculation banti hai: 250 / 750 = 1/3, jiska matlab hai exactly 33.33% profit.

2. Successive Discounts:
Agar lagataar 3 discounts (20%, 25%, 20%) mil rahe hon, toh ek-ek karke minus karne mein time waste na karein. ₹100 ka multiplier use karein.

Detailed Logic: 100 * 0.80 * 0.75 * 0.80 = 48. Yani ₹100 ki original cheez finally ₹48 ki milegi. Total discount = 100 - 48 = 52%.

3. Reverse Alphabet Coding & Memory:

Coding Detail: A to Z ki ultee (reverse) ranking nikalne ka shortcut hai: 27 - Forward Rank. Jaise Z ka forward rank 26 hai, toh reverse rank 27 - 26 = 1 hoga.

Computer Detail: Sabse fastest memory jo CPU ke andar hoti hai woh CPU Registers hain. Aur DRAM aisi volatile memory hai jise data bachane ke liye thousands of times per second electrical refresh chahiye.

4. J&K Reorganisation Act (2019):

GK Detail: Is act ki original aur basic structure mein exactly 14 Parts aur 5 Schedules banaye gaye the.

Day 2: Ratios, Syllogisms & Rivers
1. Age Ratios (The Unit Jump Method):

Detailed Logic: Agar Aman aur Raj ki age ka ratio 3:4 hai, aur 5 saal baad 4:5 ho jata hai. Dhyan se dekhein toh 3 se 4, aur 4 se 5 — dono taraf exactly 1 unit ka jump hai. Iska matlab hai 1 unit = 5 saal. Aman ki present age (3 units) nikalne ke liye: 3 * 5 = 15 saal.

2. Compound Ratio & Simplification Trap:

Detailed Logic: Agar A:B aur B:C diya ho toh A:C nikalte waqt ek strict rule yaad rakhna hai — JKSSB exams hamesha lowest simplified fraction mangte hain. Agar calculation se 6:20 aa raha hai, toh usey wahi mat chhodiye, cut karke hamesha 3:10 par laana hai warna 0.5 marks cut ho jayenge.

3. Syllogisms (Trapped Circle Rule):

Detailed Logic: Statements ko draw karte waqt hamesha "Minimal Overlap" draw karein. Agar statement hai "No Pencil is Eraser", toh Eraser ke circle ko hamesha bilkul alag aur door rakhein taaki visual confusion na ho.

4. J&K Rivers & Dams:

GK Detail: Salal, Baglihar, aur Dul Hasti — yeh teeno massive aur important hydroelectric projects Chenab River par banaye gaye hain.

Day 3: Time & Work, Directions & History
1. Time & Work (The LCM Chocolate Box Method):

Fractions (jaise 1/x) ko completely bhool jayein. Total work nikalne ke liye hamesha dino ka LCM lein.

Detailed Logic: Agar Rahul ek kaam 12 din mein karta hai aur Sahil 24 din mein, toh Total Work LCM (12, 24) = 24 chocolates hoga.

Rahul ki speed: 24 / 12 = 2 chocolates/day. Sahil ki speed: 24 / 24 = 1 chocolate/day. Dono milkar daily 3 chocolates khayenge. Total time = 24 / 3 = 8 din.

2. Direction Sense (Pythagorean Triplets):

Detailed Logic: Jab bhi straight-line shortest distance pucha jaye aur rasta 90° turn ho (jaise East jakar North mudna), toh Pythagoras formula se square roots nikalne ka time bachayein. Yeh standard triplets yaad rakhein: 3, 4 chalne par hamesha 5 aayega. 6, 8 chalne par 10 aayega, aur 5, 12 chalne par 13 aayega.

3. J&K History (Treaty of Amritsar):

GK Detail: Yeh historical treaty exactly March 16, 1846 ko sign hui thi (British East India Company aur Maharaja Gulab Singh ke beech). Is deal mein 75 Lakh Nanakshahi Rupees mein territory transfer hui thi.

Day 4: Interest Formulas, Blood Relations & MS Office
1. 2-Year CI/SI Difference Shortcut:

Jab Compound Interest aur Simple Interest ka exact 2 saal ka difference pucha jaye, toh dono ko alag-alag calculate mat karein. Direct formula lagayein:

Difference=P(

 )=100
2. Blood Relations (The "My" Backward Strategy):

Detailed Logic: "Pointing to a photograph" wale questions mein sentence ko English ki tarah left-to-right mat padhein. Sentence mein word "My" dhoondein aur wahan se step-by-step peeche (backward) logic lagayein. Jaise "the only son of my grandfather" automatically aapka "Father" ban jata hai.

3. MS Office Shortcuts:

Computer Detail: Hyperlink insert karne ka highly-tested shortcut Ctrl + K hai. Text ko page ke exact center mein align karne ke liye Ctrl + E use hota hai.

Day 5: Mixtures, Number Series & Panchayati Raj
1. Mixtures & Alligation (The Cross Method):

Jab do alag-alag rates ko milakar ek 'middle' (mean) value banani ho, toh Algebra (
x) ki jagah direct cross subtraction karein.

Detailed Logic: Agar ₹45 aur ₹55 ki sugar milakar ₹48 ka mixture banana hai, toh cross difference nikalein: (55 - 48) = 7 aur (48 - 45) = 3. Mixture ka required ratio direct aa jayega: 7:3.

2. Number Series (Double-Difference Trick):

Detailed Logic: Agar kisi series mein aapko basic squares ya cubes ka pattern nahi dikh raha, toh pehle numbers ke beech ka difference nikalein. Agar ek baar mein pattern samajh na aaye, toh difference ka bhi difference nikalein. JKSSB hamesha yeh hidden layer wala pattern puchta hai.

3. J&K Panchayati Raj Act (1989):

GK Detail: Yeh act ek 3-Tier system establish karta hai (Halqa, Block, District). Village level par Halqa Panchayat banti hai jisme minimum 7 aur maximum 11 Panches hote hain. Auraton (Women) ke liye strictly 33% seats reserved hain.

Day 6: Average Speed, Venn Diagrams & National Parks
1. Average Speed (The Equal Distance Formula):

Ek certain distance jane aur wapas aane wale questions mein dono speeds ko add karke 2 se divide karna sabse bada mathematical trap hai.

Detailed Logic: Always use the formula:

x+y
2xy

 
. Example ke liye, agar koi 60 km/hr se jata hai aur 40 km/hr se wapas aata hai, toh average speed

100
2×60×40

 =48 km/hr
hogi (50 nahi).

2. Logical Venn Diagrams:

Detailed Logic: Isme Syllogisms ki tarah imaginary circles nahi, balki real-world logic mapping lagani hai. Jaise "Vehicles, Cars, Motorcycles" — isme Cars aur Motorcycles ke circles completely alag honge, par yeh dono ek bade "Vehicles" ke circle ke andar draw kiye jayenge.

3. J&K National Parks Geography:

GK Detail: Dachigam National Park (Srinagar) globally aur exclusively state animal Hangul (Kashmir Stag) ke liye famous hai. Wahin Salim Ali National Park ka naam India ke sabse prominent ornithologist (The Birdman of India) ke samman mein rakha gaya hai.

Day 7: Profit Ratios, Alphabet Coding & Lakes
1. Profit & Loss (The MP/CP Shortcut):

Jab question mein Discount % aur Profit % diya ho, aur seedha Marked Price (MP) aur Cost Price (CP) ka ratio pucha jaye, toh Selling price bilkul mat nikalein.

Detailed Logic: Direct Formula use karein:

CP
MP

 = 
100−Discount %
100+Profit %

 
Agar 20% profit aur 20% discount hai, toh equation banegi 120 / 80, jo kat kar directly 3:2 ka ratio de degi.

2. Letter-to-Number Coding:

Detailed Logic: Jab kisi word ko ek digit mein code kiya jata hai, toh mostly unke alphabet ranks add kiye jate hain. Ranks instantly nikalne ke liye hamesha E-J-O-T-Y (5 - 10 - 15 - 20 - 25) trick yaad rakhein.

3. J&K Lakes Geography:

GK Detail: Wular Lake tectonic activity se form hui hai aur ise Jhelum river feed karti hai. Mansar aur Surinsar ko "Twin Lakes" kaha jata hai, jinka connection Mahabharata se hai. Sabse bada catch yeh hai ki yeh dono lakes Kashmir mein nahi, balki Jammu Division mein aati hain.

Examiners love train problems because they test whether you catch unit mismatches before calculating.

The 5/18 Speed Converter: Train lengths are given in meters, but speeds are almost always in 
km/hr. Convert speed to 
m/s immediately:

Speed (m/s)=Speed (km/hr)× 
18

18
5

 =3×5=15 m/s

72 km/hr× 
18
5

 =4×5=20 m/s

90 km/hr× 
18
5

 =5×5=25 m/s

Distance Rules:

Crossing a Pole / Tree / Standing Person: Neglect their width.

Total Distance=Length of Train
Crossing a Platform / Bridge / Tunnel: Add both lengths.

Total Distance=Length of Train+Length of Platform
Time Formula: 
Time= 
Speed (in m/s)

2. General Intelligence & Reasoning: Dice (Clockwise Rule)
When an examiner shows two different orientations of the same dice sharing one common number:

Identify the common number on both views.

Write down the numbers clockwise starting from the common number for Dice 1.

Directly underneath, write down the numbers clockwise starting from the common number for Dice 2.

The pairs that line up vertically are exact opposite faces.

Plaintext
Example:
Dice 1 shows: 3 - 5 - 2
Dice 2 shows: 3 - 1 - 6

Dice 1 (Clockwise from 3):  3  ->  5  ->  2
Dice 2 (Clockwise from 3):  3  ->  1  ->  6
                             |      |      |
Opposite Pairs:           (3 - 4) (5 - 1)(2 - 6)
(Since 4 is the only unseen number, 3 must be opposite 4).

3. General Awareness: High-Yield J&K Fairs & Festivals
Jhiri Mela (Jammu): Annual fair held at village Jhiri (Kanachak), Jammu. It pays tribute to Baba Jitto, a legendary Dogra farmer who sacrificed his life protesting against the oppressive exploitation of feudal landlords.

Mela Kheer Bhawani (Kashmir): Celebrated annually on Jesht Ashtami at the sacred shrine of Ragnya Devi located at Tulmulla in the Ganderbal district. It represents one of the most revered cultural gatherings of the Kashmiri Pandit community.

Day 9 Masterclass: Pipes & Cisterns, Order & Ranking, J&K Forts & Palaces
1. Quantitative Aptitude: Pipes & Cisterns (The Negative Work Concept)
Pipes & Cisterns uses the exact same LCM Chocolate Box method as Time & Work, with one twist: an emptying pipe (leak/outlet) performs negative work.

Rule: Assign filling pipes a positive 
(+) efficiency and emptying pipes a negative 
(−) efficiency.

Worked Example: Pipe A fills a tank in 
10 hours, and Pipe B empties it in 
15 hours. If both are opened together, in how many hours will the tank fill?

Total Capacity (LCM): 
LCM(10,15)=30 units.

Efficiencies:

Pipe A (Fill) = 
+(30/10)=+3 units/hr

Pipe B (Empty) = 
−(30/15)=−2 units/hr

Net Efficiency: 
+3−2=+1 unit/hr.

Time to Fill: 
1
30

 =30 hours.

2. General Intelligence & Reasoning: Order & Ranking
Examiners frequently ask for total strength or an individual's position from the opposite end in a linear row.

Finding Total Persons: When one person's rank is given from both ends:

Total=(Rank from Left)+(Rank from Right)−1
(We subtract 1 because that same person was counted twice).

Finding Position from Other End:

Rank from Other End=(Total Persons−Given Rank)+1
Worked Example: In a row of 40 students, Rohan is 
13 
th
  from the left end. What is his position from the right end?

Rank from Right=(40−13)+1=27+1=28 
th
 
3. General Awareness: Historical Forts & Palaces of J&K
JKSSB tests builder attribution and location facts:

Bahu Fort (Jammu): Believed to be originally built by Raja Bahulochan over 
3,000 years ago on the banks of the Tawi River and refurbished by the Dogra rulers. Houses the famous Bave Wali Mata temple (Kali).

Hari Parbat Fort (Srinagar): Also known as Kooh-e-Maran. The outer rampart was built by Mughal Emperor Akbar in 1590, while the present fort atop the hill was constructed in 1808 by Afghan governor Atta Mohammad Khan.

Mubarak Mandi Palace (Jammu): The royal seat of the Dogra dynasty until 1925, showcasing a blend of Rajasthani, Mughal, and European architecture. Includes the famous Pink Hall (now Dogra Art Museum).

क्या मैं इस पूरे कन्वर्सेशन में कुछ रूल्स सेट कर सकता हूं? अगर मैं ऐसा है तो ये पॉसिबल है मुझे ये करना है कि मैं इसमें ये रूल होना चाहिए कि एटलीस्ट किसी क्वेश्चन को अगर रीजनिंग या वो पहला या दूसरा टॉपिक होता है ना, उसका मुझे कम से कम दो-तीन से तीन-चार क्वेश्चन पूछो। मतलब तीन-चार क्वेश्चन एक्सप्लेन करो विद एग्जांपल्स ना। तो मुझे थोड़ा समझ आए, थोड़ी इसमें भी डिफिकल्टी होनी चाहिए थोड़ी तो मुझे समझ आए ताकि मैं फिर बाद में इजीली वो बात कर सकूं। तो इस हिसाब से मुझे डे 8 एंड डे 9 फिर से अलग-अलग डे वाइज का मुझे दो ताकि मैं चीजें फिर से रिपीट करूं।

क्या मैं इस पूरे कन्वर्सेशन में कुछ रूल्स सेट कर सकता हूं? अगर मैं ऐसा है तो ये पॉसिबल है मुझे ये करना है कि मैं इसमें ये रूल होना चाहिए कि एटलीस्ट किसी क्वेश्चन को अगर रीजनिंग या वो पहला या दूसरा टॉपिक होता है ना, उसका मुझे कम से कम दो-तीन से तीन-चार क्वेश्चन पूछो। मतलब तीन-चार क्वेश्चन एक्सप्लेन करो विद एग्जांपल्स ना। तो मुझे थोड़ा समझ आए, थोड़ी इसमें भी डिफिकल्टी होनी चाहिए थोड़ी तो मुझे समझ आए ताकि मैं फिर बाद में इजीली वो बात कर सकूं। तो इस हिसाब से मुझे डे 8 एंड डे 9 फिर से अलग-अलग डे वाइज का मुझे दो ताकि मैं चीजें फिर से रिपीट करूं।

हाँ, बिल्कुल! यह आपका स्टडी प्लान है और इसे आपकी ज़रूरतों के हिसाब से ही चलना चाहिए।

अब से हमारा यही नियम रहेगा: क्वांट और रीज़निंग के हर टॉपिक में केवल एक बेसिक फॉर्मूला नहीं, बल्कि कम से कम 3 से 4 अलग-अलग डिफिकल्टी लेवल (बेसिक से लेकर ट्रिकी/एग्जाम लेवल) वाले सॉल्व्ड उदाहरण दिए जाएंगे।

विषय 2: General Intelligence & Reasoning — Dice (पासा)
उदाहरण 1 (Level 1: 1 कॉमन नंबर — Clockwise Rule)
प्रश्न: एक पासे की दो स्थितियां दी गई हैं:

स्थिति 1: 3, 5, 2

स्थिति 2: 3, 1, 6
संख्या 5 के विपरीत कौन-सी संख्या होगी?

हल:

दोनों में कॉमन संख्या 3 है।

3 से शुरू करके घड़ी की सुई की दिशा (Clockwise) में लिखें:

पासा 1: 
3→5→2

पासा 2: 
3→1→6

ऊपर-नीचे आने वाली संख्याएं एक-दूसरे के विपरीत होती हैं:

5 के विपरीत 1

2 के विपरीत 6

3 के विपरीत बची हुई संख्या 4

उदाहरण 2 (Level 2: 2 कॉमन नंबर का नियम)
प्रश्न: एक पासे की दो स्थितियां दी गई हैं:

स्थिति 1: 2, 4, 1

स्थिति 2: 2, 4, 6
संख्या 1 के विपरीत कौन-सी संख्या होगी?

नियम: यदि दोनों स्थितियों में दो संख्याएं समान (Common) हों, तो बची हुई तीसरी संख्याएं सीधे एक-दूसरे के विपरीत होती हैं।

हल:

दोनों में संख्याएं 2 और 4 कॉमन हैं।

पासा 1 में बची संख्या: 1

पासा 2 में बची संख्या: 6

अत: 1 के विपरीत सीधे 6 होगा।

उदाहरण 3 (Level 3: मानक पासा / Standard Dice Rule)
प्रश्न: एक मानक पासे (Standard Dice) में संख्या 3 के विपरीत फलक (face) पर कौन-सा अंक होगा?

नियम: एक मानक पासे में विपरीत फलकों का योग हमेशा 7 होता है।

हल:

विपरीत अंक=7−3=4
(याद रखें: 1 के विपरीत 6, 2 के विपरीत 5, और 3 के विपरीत 4 होता है)।

उदाहरण 4 (Level 4: खुला पासा / Open Dice Folding)
प्रश्न: एक खुला पासा इस क्रम में लंबवत (vertical) दिया गया है: 
[1]−[2]−[3]−[4], और साइड में 2 के दाईं ओर 
[5] और 3 के बाईं ओर 
[6] जुड़ा है। 2 के विपरीत क्या होगा?

नियम (एकांतर नियम / Alternate Face Rule): खुले पासे में एक खाना छोड़कर अगला खाना हमेशा उसका विपरीत होता है।

हल:

1 का विपरीत = 3 (एक खाना छोड़कर)

2 का विपरीत = 4 (एक खाना छोड़कर)

बचे हुए बाहरी फलक एक-दूसरे के विपरीत होते हैं: 5 का विपरीत = 6

अत: 2 के विपरीत 4 होगा।

विषय 3: General Awareness — J&K के प्रमुख मेले और त्यौहार
झिरी मेला (Jhiri Mela):

स्थान: जम्मू जिले के कानाचक (Kanachak) के पास झिरी गांव में हर साल कार्तिक पूर्णिमा पर लगता है।

इतिहास: यह मेला लोक नायक और ईमानदार किसान बाबा जित्तो (Baba Jitto) के बलिदान की याद में मनाया जाता है, जिन्होंने सामंतवादी ज़मींदार की नाइंसाफ़ी के खिलाफ़ अपनी जान दे दी थी।

खीर भवानी मेला (Mela Kheer Bhawani):

स्थान: कश्मीर के गांदरबल (Ganderbal) जिले के तुलमुल्ला (Tulmulla) गांव में।

महत्व: यह मंदिर देवी रागन्या देवी को समर्पित है और कश्मीरी पंडित समुदाय का सबसे बड़ा और पवित्र वार्षिक त्यौहार है (ज्येष्ठ अष्टमी पर आयोजित)।

पुरमंडल मेला (Purmandal Mela):

स्थान: सांबा/जम्मू के पास देविका नदी के तट पर।

महत्व: पुरमंडल को "छोटा काशी" भी कहा जाता है। यह मेला महाशिवरात्रि के अवसर पर लगता है।

Day 8: Trains, Dice Rules एवं J&K सामान्य ज्ञान
1. Quantitative Aptitude — Trains & Platforms (ट्रेन और प्लेटफॉर्म)
मूलभूत नियम (यूनिट कन्वर्जन): गति यदि 
km/hr में हो, तो उसे मीटर प्रति सेकंड (
m/s) में बदलने के लिए

5

  से गुणा करें:

प्लेटफॉर्म, पुल अथवा सुरंग को पार करने में कुल दूरी = ट्रेन की लंबाई + प्लेटफॉर्म/पुल की लंबाई

Level 1 (मूल स्तर — खंभा पार करना)

Level 3 (कठिन स्तर — ट्रेन की लंबाई ज्ञात करना)
प्रश्न: 90 km/hr की गति से दौड़ रही एक रेलगाड़ी 250 मीटर लंबे प्लेटफॉर्म को 22 सेकंड में पूरी तरह पार कर लेती है। ट्रेन की वास्तविक लंबाई ज्ञात कीजिए।

2. General Intelligence & Reasoning — Dice (पासा)
Level 1 (एक उभयनिष्ठ संख्या — Clockwise Rule)
प्रश्न: एक पासे की दो स्थितियाँ हैं:

स्थिति 1: 3, 5, 2

स्थिति 2: 3, 1, 6

संख्या 5 के विपरीत फलक पर कौन-सी संख्या होगी?

हल: दोनों में कॉमन संख्या 3 है। 3 से शुरू करके घड़ी की दिशा (Clockwise) में लिखें:

पासा 1: 
3→5→2

पासा 2: 
3→1→6

ऊपर-नीचे आने वाली संख्याएँ परस्पर विपरीत होती हैं: 5 के विपरीत 1 और 2 के विपरीत 6 (तथा 3 के विपरीत शेष बची संख्या 4 होगी)।

Level 2 (दो उभयनिष्ठ संख्याएँ — Two Common Rule)
प्रश्न: पासे की दो स्थितियाँ दी गई हैं:

स्थिति 1: 2, 4, 1

स्थिति 2: 2, 4, 6

संख्या 1 के विपरीत फलक पर कौन-सा अंक होगा?

नियम: यदि दोनों स्थितियों में दो अंक समान हों, तो दोनों पासों के बचे हुए तीसरे अंक सीधे एक-दूसरे के विपरीत होते हैं।

हल: यहाँ 2 और 4 दोनों पासों में कॉमन हैं। अतः बचा हुआ अंक 1 सीधे 6 के विपरीत होगा।

Level 3 (मानक पासा नियम — Standard Dice)
प्रश्न: एक मानक पासे में फलक 3 के विपरीत फलक पर कौन-सा अंक अंकित होगा?

नियम: मानक पासे के किन्हीं भी दो विपरीत फलकों का योग सदैव 7 होता है।

हल: 
7−3=4 (अर्थात 1 के विपरीत 6, 2 के विपरीत 5, और 3 के विपरीत 4 होता है)।

Level 4 (खुला पासा — Open Dice Folding)
प्रश्न: एक खुले पासे में एक सीधी खड़ी पट्टी में ऊपर से नीचे क्रम में अंक 1, 2, 3, 4 दिए गए हैं; 2 के दाईं ओर 5 और 3 के बाईं ओर 6 जुड़ा है। पासे को मोड़ने पर 2 के विपरीत क्या आएगा?

नियम (एकांतर फलक नियम): एक सीधी रेखा में एक खाना छोड़कर अगला खाना उसका विपरीत फलक होता है।

हल:

1 का विपरीत = 3

2 का विपरीत = 4

बाहरी फलक परस्पर विपरीत होंगे: 5 का विपरीत = 6

अतः 2 के विपरीत 4 होगा।

3. General Awareness — J&K के प्रमुख मेले एवं सांस्कृतिक धरोहर
झिरी मेला (Jhiri Mela):

यह ऐतिहासिक मेला जम्मू जिले के कानाचक के पास झिरी गाँव में कार्तिक पूर्णिमा पर लगता है।

यह स्थानीय किसान बाबा जित्तो के बलिदान की स्मृति में आयोजित होता है, जिन्होंने सामंतवादी ज़मींदारों के अन्यायपूर्ण शोषण के विरुद्ध अपने प्राण त्याग दिए थे।

खीर भवानी मेला (Mela Kheer Bhawani):

यह पवित्र मेला कश्मीर के गांदरबल जिले के तुलमुल्ला गाँव में स्थित रागन्या देवी मंदिर परिसर में आयोजित होता है।

यह ज्येष्ठ अष्टमी के पावन अवसर पर कश्मीरी पंडित समुदाय द्वारा मनाया जाने वाला सबसे प्रमुख वार्षिक उत्सव है।

पुरमंडल मेला (Purmandal Mela):

सांबा/जम्मू जिले के पास पवित्र देविका नदी के तट पर महाशिवरात्रि पर यह मेला लगता है।

धार्मिक महत्व के कारण इस स्थल को ऐतिहासिक रूप से "छोटा काशी" की संज्ञा दी गई है।

Day 8 — टेस्ट ड्रिल
सही उत्तर: +2 अंक | गलत उत्तर: -0.5 अंक

लक्ष्य: 10 / 10 अंक

Q1. Quantitative Aptitude (Train vs Bridge)

200 मीटर लंबी एक रेलगाड़ी 72 km/hr की नियत गति से दौड़ रही है। 300 मीटर लंबे नदी पुल को पूरी तरह पार करने में उसे कितने सेकंड का समय लगेगा?

A) 20 सेकंड

B) 25 सेकंड

C) 30 सेकंड

D) 15 सेकंड

Q2. Quantitative Aptitude (Finding Train Length)

एक रेलगाड़ी 54 km/hr की गति से चलते हुए सिग्नल के खंभे को 18 सेकंड में पार कर लेती है। उस रेलगाड़ी की कुल लंबाई कितनी है?

A) 250 मीटर

B) 270 मीटर

C) 300 मीटर

D) 220 मीटर

Q3. General Intelligence & Reasoning (Two Common Rule)

एक ही पासे के दो अलग-अलग दृश्य नीचे दर्शाए गए हैं:

दृश्य 1: 3, 5, 2

दृश्य 2: 3, 5, 6

संख्या 2 वाले फलक के ठीक विपरीत फलक पर कौन-सी संख्या होगी?

A) 6

B) 1

C) 4

D) 3

Q4. General Intelligence & Reasoning (Clockwise Rule)

एक पासे की दो स्थितियाँ दी गई हैं:

स्थिति 1: 1, 4, 6

स्थिति 2: 1, 3, 5

संख्या 4 के ठीक विपरीत फलक पर कौन-सा अंक आएगा?

A) 5

B) 3

C) 2

D) 6

Q5. General Awareness (J&K Heritage)

जम्मू और कश्मीर के किस ऐतिहासिक तीर्थ स्थल को 'छोटा काशी' के नाम से जाना जाता है, जहाँ महाशिवरात्रि का वार्षिक मेला लगता है?

A) कटरा

B) पुरमंडल

C) झिरी

D) सुधमहादेव

इन 5 प्रश्नों को अपनी रफ़ शीट पर हल करके अपने विकल्प (A, B, C...) दर्ज करें।

Section 2: General Intelligence & Reasoning — Dice Rules
Example 1 (Level 1: Single Common Face — Clockwise Rule)
Question: Ek dice ke do views diye gaye hain:

View 1 faces: 3, 5, 2

View 2 faces: 3, 1, 6
Number 5 ke opposite face par kaun sa number aayega?

Step-by-step Solution:

Dono views mein common number 3 hai.

Number 3 se start karke clockwise direction mein numbers likhein:

Dice 1: 
3→5→2

Dice 2: 
3→1→6

Pairs vertical match hote hain:

5 ke opposite = 1

2 ke opposite = 6

3 ke opposite bacha hua number = 4

Answer: 5 ke opposite 1 hoga.

Example 2 (Level 2: Two Common Faces Rule)
Question: Ek dice ke do alag views hain:

View 1 faces: 2, 4, 1

View 2 faces: 2, 4, 6
Number 1 ke opposite kaun sa number hoga?

Rule: Agar do positions mein 2 numbers common hon, toh bache hue single numbers directly ek-doosre ke opposite hote hain.

Step-by-step Solution:

Common numbers: 2 aur 4.

View 1 mein bacha: 1

View 2 mein bacha: 6

Answer: 1 ke opposite seedha 6 hoga.

Example 3 (Level 3: Standard Dice Rule)
Question: Ek standard dice mein number 3 ke opposite face par kaun sa number hota hai?

Rule: Standard dice mein kisi bhi do opposite faces ka sum hamesha 7 hota hai.

Step-by-step Solution:

Opposite Face=7−3=4
(Pairs: 1 opposite 6, 2 opposite 5, 3 opposite 4).

Example 4 (Level 4: Open Dice Folding Rule)
Question: Ek open dice vertical strip mein diya hai jisme boxes 
[1]−[2]−[3]−[4] upar se niche hain; 
[5] box 2 ke right side par hai aur 
[6] box 3 ke left side par hai. Fold karne par number 2 ke opposite kaun sa number aayega?

Rule (Alternate Face Rule): Ek straight line mein ek box chhodkar agla box uska opposite face hota hai.

Step-by-step Solution:

1 ka opposite = 3 (ek box chhodkar)

2 ka opposite = 4 (ek box chhodkar)

External flaps: 5 ka opposite = 6

Answer: 2 ke opposite 4 aayega.

Section 3: General Awareness — J&K Fairs & Festivals
Jhiri Mela (Jammu):

Location: Kanachak ke paas Jhiri village (Jammu district).

Context: Yeh mela har saal Kartik Purnima par lagta hai. Yeh Dogra peasant-farmer Baba Jitto ke supreme sacrifice ko honor karne ke liye manaya jata hai, jinhone feudal landlords ke oppressive exploitation ke khilaf ladte hue apni jaan de di thi.

Mela Kheer Bhawani (Kashmir):

Location: Tulmulla shrine, Ganderbal district.

Context: Goddess Ragnya Devi ke temple mein Jesht Ashtami ke din manaya jata hai. Yeh Kashmiri Pandit community ka sabse sacred aur major annual festival hai.

Purmandal Mela (Jammu):

Location: Samba/Jammu ke paas sacred Devika river ke banks par.

Context: Is religious shrine ko historical traditions mein "Chhota Kashi" ke naam se jana jata hai. Yeh mela har saal Mahashivratri par organize hota hai.

Section 1: Quantitative Aptitude — Pipes & Cisterns
Pipes & Cisterns follows the exact same LCM Total Capacity method as Time & Work, with one fundamental rule:

Inlet Pipes (Filling): Positive work (
+).

Outlet Pipes / Leaks (Emptying): Negative work (
−).

Time to Fill/Empty= 
Net Efficiency
Total Capacity (LCM)

18 hours. If both pipes are opened simultaneously in an empty tank, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?

Total Capacity: 
LCM(12,18)=36 units.

Efficiencies:

Pipe A (Fill) = 
+ 
12
36

 =+3 units/hr

Pipe B (Empty) = 
− 
18
36

 =−2 units/hr

Net Rate: 
+3−2=+1 unit/hr.

Time Required: 
1
36

 =36 hours.

 =−1 unit/hr

nd

Example 4 (Level 4: Overlapping / Minimum Strength Case)
Problem: In a line, Amit is 
12 
th
  from the left end, and Sumit is 
15 
th
  from the right end. There are 
4 persons sitting strictly between them. What is the minimum number of persons who could be in this line?

Rule (Overlapping Formula): In an overlapping arrangement (where the two individuals cross each other):

Minimum Total=(Left Rank+Right Rank)−(Persons Between)−2
(We subtract 2 because Amit and Sumit are counted twice in the overlapping segment).

Calculation:

Minimum Total=(12+15)−4−2=27−6=21 persons
Section 3: General Awareness — Historical Forts & Palaces of J&K
Bahu Fort (Jammu):

Location & River: Perched on a sandstone plateau overlooking the left bank of the Tawi River.

History: Originally built by Raja Bahulochan over 
3,000 years ago; later extensively renovated and fortified by the Dogra rulers.

Significance: Houses the revered Bave Wali Mata temple (dedicated to Goddess Mahakali). The terraced Bagh-e-Bahu garden surrounds the fort.

Hari Parbat Fort (Kooh-e-Maran, Srinagar):

History: The outer fortification wall was constructed by Mughal Emperor Akbar in 1590. The present fort standing atop the hill was built in 1808 by Afghan governor Atta Mohammad Khan.

Significance: Revered across communities; houses the Sharika Devi Temple, the shrine of Makhdoom Sahib, and Gurdwara Chatti Patshahi at its base.

Mubarak Mandi Palace (Jammu):

History: Served as the royal residential seat of the Dogra dynasty until 1925.

Architecture: A rare blend of Rajasthani, Mughal, and European Baroque/Gothic styles. Houses the Pink Hall, which today serves as the Dogra Art Museum (famous for Kangra and Pahari miniature paintings).

Akhnoor Fort (Jammu):

Location: Perched on the right bank of the Chenab River.

History: Construction was initiated by Raja Tegh Singh in 1762 and completed by Raja Alam Singh in 1802. Close to the significant Buddhist archaeological site of Ambaran.

Bhimgarh Fort (Reasi):

History: Often referred to as Reasi Fort. Originally constructed using clay by Raja Bhimdev Rasyal; later rebuilt using solid stone by Maharaja Gulab Singh and utilized strategically by General Zorawar Singh.

Q1. Quantitative Aptitude (Pipe Switched Off Early)
Pipe A can fill a tank in 
10 minutes, and Pipe B can fill it in 
15 minutes. Both pipes are opened together. After 
2 minutes, Pipe A is suddenly turned off. What is the total time taken to fill the tank?
(Hint: Total Capacity = LCM of 10 and 15. Calculate how much work Pipe A did in its 2 minutes. The rest of the work MUST be done by Pipe B. Divide the remaining work by Pipe B's efficiency to get the total time Pipe B was running).
A) 
10 minutes
B) 
12 minutes
C) 
14 minutes
D) 
8 minutes

Drop your single answer (A, B, C, or D) for this question. Once you clear this, we will officially stamp Day 9 as mastered and roll out the 4-level deep dive for Day 10!

check again please Q2

Let's trace the exact timeline of the cistern filling minute-by-minute to see the precise math.

Step 1: The Total Capacity
The LCM of 15 and 20 is 60. We will assume the cistern holds a total of 60 units of water.

Pipe X Efficiency: 
60/15=4 units/minute

Pipe Y Efficiency: 
60/20=3 units/minute

Step 2: Phase 1 (Both pipes open for 4 minutes)
When both pipes run together, they fill 
(4+3)=7 units/minute.
In the first 4 minutes, they fill: 
7×4=28 units.

Step 3: The Remaining Work
The total capacity is 60 units.
Remaining water needed: 
60−28=32 units.

Step 4: Phase 2 (Pipe Y works alone)
Pipe X is turned off. Pipe Y must fill the remaining 32 units alone at its speed of 
3 units/minute.
Time taken by Y alone: 
32/3=10 
3
2

  minutes.
To convert that fraction into exact seconds: 
3
2

  of a 
60-second minute is exactly 
40 seconds.
So, Phase 2 takes exactly 
10 minutes 40 seconds.

Step 5: Total Time Calculation
Phase 1 (
4 minutes) + Phase 2 (
10 minutes 40 seconds) = 
14 minutes 40 seconds.

The previous evaluation stands. The mathematically exact answer is Option D (
14 minutes 40 seconds), not Option B (
15 minutes). It is incredibly common to miscalculate the fractional remaining time when splitting work into two different phases, which is exactly why examiners intentionally put 
15 minutes in the options as a trap.

Now that you can see exactly where those remaining 32 units went, let's crush the pending Reinforcement Mini-Drill to prove you have permanently mastered this fractional time trap.
